Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Pipeline Integrity Gate Station Upgrades 
Project/Program Manager: Jim Buonincontri 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 2024 
U. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $2,000 
O&M:  
 
V.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
W. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
X. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
The objective of this project is to replace all pipe within four Orange and Rockland gate stations that 
operate at 20% or more of Specified Minimum Yield Strength (SMYS) in order to comply with the new 
pipeline integrity requirements added to 49 CFR 192 by the PHMSA Final Rule, Pipeline Safety: Safety 
of Gas Transmission Pipelines (“2019 Final Rule”), issued on October 1, 2019. By replacing this pipe, 
lower stress levels, lower risk, and improved pipeline safety will result. This will also eliminate the 
need to monitor the stations to determine if a High Consequence Area (“HCA”) develops and would 
eliminate the need to comply with new integrity requirements (e.g.,  MAOP Reconfirmation, Expansion 
of Assessment Requirements, Verification of Pipeline Material Properties and Attributes, and other 
related amendments). If the pipe is not replaced and an HCA is developed, the entirety of 255.901-
255.951 would apply. If an HCA did not develop, additional stringent requirements would apply to 
non-HCA and Moderate Consequence Area (MCA) pipelines due to the new regulation. 
 
Orange and Rockland owns and operates approximately 5,000 feet of transmission pipeline (i.e., pipe 
operating at or over 20% of SMYS) located along Route 210 in the Town of Stony Point, Rockland 
County. In addition, there are short segments of transmission pipeline of various sizes and locations at 
the Tappan, Greenwood Lake, Buena Vista and Pearl River gate stations. Total pipe at all four stations 
is approximately 1,500 feet. 
 
The transmission pipe at each of these stations is not contiguous and is both above and below ground.  
The maximum allowed operating pressure (“MAOP”) of these segments are 1,083 psig for Tappan and 
Pearl River, and 920 psig for Greenwood Lake and Buena Vista.  Operating stress based on MAOP 
ranges from approximately 21% to 36.6%.  
 
As of the filing date, there are no HCAs associated with any of these transmission facilities. The 
Company has concluded this by identifying HCAs using the potential impact circle (“PIC”) method 


--- Page 123 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 56 OF 141 
 
 
 
56 
detailed in 255.903(e)(2), (f), (g) and (h). This method must be employed on a continuous basis and 
largely consists of identifying places of congregation in close proximity to any parts of these lines.  
Presently, because no HCAs exist, the Company is not required to have a full integrity management 
program that complies with all sections of the transmission pipeline integrity management regulations 
in Part 255.  However, there are aspects of the regulations that apply (e.g., 255.911(p) and PHMSA 
Advisory Bulletins such as ADB-2016-07) which entail monitoring integrity conditions of the 
transmission lines and monitoring the physical area around them to determine if any HCAs have 
developed. If one does develop, the new HCA must be incorporated into a baseline integrity 
assessment plan and the integrity assessment must be conducted (255.905(c), 255.919 and 255.921(f)).  
 
Additionally, the new integrity regulation in the 2019 Final Rule stipulates additional requirements 
that apply to transmission lines. These include conducting integrity assessments on non-HCA pipeline, 
reconfirming MAOP pressures tests where adequate documentation does not exist, performing threat 
analysis, risk based prioritization scheduling, integrity assessments, periodic reassessments including 
remediations as stipulated for HCAs and verification of material properties and attributes that includes 
a program to conduct both destructive and non-destructive testing for line pipe and pipeline 
components. 
 
Replacing transmission pipe with non-transmission rated pipe will not only reduce system risk, but 
also avoids the need to comply with the above mentioned new integrity regulation requirements and 
the need to confirm non-HCA status which is a requirement of the initial integrity regulation.  
 
The Company is planning to continue with this program (begun under the current Gas Rate Plan) at 
the Greenwood Lake Station and Buena Vista Station. The Company is expected to complete the 
replacement of pipe at the Tappan Station in 2021.  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
Currently, all Orange and Rockland transmission pipe is in non-High Consequence Areas (“non-
HCAs”). For non-HCA transmission pipe, regulations require operators to implement measures to 
assure that increases or changes in housing or in places of congregation, indoors and outdoors, 
surrounding any parts of these lines, do not change the status of the line to HCA. If a change in status 
occurs, Orange and Rockland must implement the full Transmission Integrity Management Program 
(“TIMP”) regulation including 255.901 through 255.951. This can be avoided if the Company replaces 
pipe over 20% of SMYS with lower stress pipe.  
 
Additionally, as discussed above, the changes to 49 CFR 192 in the 2019 Final Rule  increased the 
requirements for non-HCA pipe and HCA pipe  and expands requirements to include performing 
assessments such as in-line inspection, hydrostatic testing, external corrosion direct assessment, and 
internal corrosion direct assessment on non-HCA pipe, as well as the other requirements mentioned 
previously. A requirement such as this would necessitate a large expansion to the Company’s integrity 
program, with a commensurate increase in funds. Replacing this pipe will eliminate both risk and an 
expansion of the integrity program.  
 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
The replacement of existing transmission pipe will reduce system risk and it will support the 
mitigation of Enterprise Risk Management (“ERMs”) for Gas Transmission Event.  
 
 
 


--- Page 124 ---

2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
As an alternative, the gate stations that have transmission pipe could be derated therefore eliminating 
transmission piping within the stations, eliminating risk of operating older vintage transmission pipe, 
and eliminating the requirements of the transmission integrity regulations. This would necessitate new 
stations to be built in order to deliver gas to the present customers. No transmission piping would be 
installed in the new stations and all code requirements would be followed, as applicable to non-
transmission gas piping. This alternative would cost considerably more money and take significantly 
more time to implement than the proposed solution, therefore it was rejected.

Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Middletown ROV 
Project/Program Manager: Alfred Gaddi 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 10003211 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☒ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated Date In Service: 12/31/22 
W. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $700 
O&M:  
 
X.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
Y. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
Z. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company will install a remotely operated valve (“ROV”) on the 12-inch line in the vicinity of 
Route 6 in Middletown, NY.  This work will include easement acquisition, surveys, drawings, and the 
purchase of materials, valve and pipe installation, and electronics and communications for remote 
operation.  The installation of approximately 400 feet of 12-inch wrapped steel pipe, related fittings, 
and 12-inch valves will also be required in order to complete this project. 
 
ROVs are operated by the system operator in the Gas Control Center, giving the Company the ability 
to remotely isolate a section of the extra high pressure (“XHP”) system in the event of a major pipeline 
incident.  By having this ability, the Company is able to minimize the hazard of blowing gas and its 
consequences (both safety and environmental). 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Currently, the Company does not have an ROV located between the California Ave and Middletown 
stations, a distance of approximately 4.5 miles.  Should the 12-inch line on Route 6 ever be damaged, 
the ROV and associated piping will allow Orange and Rockland to isolate the section of damaged pipe 
while still maintaining flow to the California Ave or Middletown station.  Currently, if the 12-inch line 
is damaged on Route 6 or if the 12-inch line on Rt 284 from Westtown to Middletown is damaged, both 
the California Ave and Middletown station would be lost, impacting approximately 13,400 customers 
in the Middletown area. 
 
The installation of a ROV provides Orange and Rockland the advantage of quickly isolating segments 
of the 12-inch extra high pressure line in an emergency by allowing remote operation of the valve as 


--- Page 128 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 61 OF 141 
 
 
 
61 
opposed to having to dispatch a crew to manually close the valve.  This quicker isolation minimizes the 
amount of natural gas venting to the atmosphere (and the associated methane emissions), reducing any 
possible damage to the surroundings, and reducing the number of customers affected.  
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Installing ROVs is consistent with Orange and Rockland’s long-range plans and risk management as it 
increases public and employee safety and achieves operational excellence by improving response time 
for addressing gas emergencies.  
  
Orange and Rockland’s Installation of ROVs is also a mitigative measure identified in two of Orange 
and Rockland’s Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) programs: Gas System Fire Explosion and Large-
Scale Customer Outage. 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
Do not install a new ROV and use existing ROVs or manual main valves already located on the system 
to isolate the section of gas main if an emergency occurs. If the Company were to proceed with this 
alternative the Company would lose two regulator stations in Middletown if a gas emergency were to 
occur at this location.  This would result in a significant number of customers being without gas 
service, which would be particularly detrimental if it were to occur during the heating season.  
Additionally, if existing manual valves were to be used during an emergency at this location, it would 
take Company crews significantly more time to isolate the main and eliminate the gas emergency.  
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
n/a

• 
Improve Company response time for gas emergencies 


--- Page 129 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 62 OF 141 
 
 
 
62 
• 
Minimizing the emergency venting of natural gas to the atmosphere (and the associated 
methane emissions)

Due to the risk associated with Orange and Rockland not installing a new ROV somewhere on the 
system between the California Ave and Middletown stations, it is important that this project be 
completed.

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Upgrade Crooked Hill Regulator Station 
Project/Program Manager: Michael Turanchik 
Project/Program Number (Level 1):  
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☒ Execution  ☒ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 1/1/18

Y. Total Funding Request ($000)

O&M:  
 
Z.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000)

($000)

Capital: 
BB. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company will remove the existing underground Crooked Hill Road regulator station and install a 
new regulator station above ground approximately 1,500 feet north of the existing location on Laurel 
Road in Pearl River, adjacent to the Pfizer right of way. The Company will incorporate current 
regulator station design standards for the new station. 
 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
The current regulator station at Crooked Hill Road feeds the area of north west Pearl River and is of 
obsolete design.  It was built in 1986 and consists of two below ground metal vaults in a residential 
neighborhood.  This project is to relocate the station aboveground, incorporating current regulator 
station design standards. 
 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Relocation of this regulator station will support the risk management goals of reliable distribution of 
gas and reduced risk of a gas-related incident. 
 
 
 


--- Page 133 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 66 OF 141 
 
 
 
66 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
One alternative was to rebuild the station in place.  This alternative was not selected because of the 
lack of necessary room in the existing vaults for the new equipment, the Company’s preference for not 
locating regulator stations below grade, when possible, and the current location in a residential 
neighborhood being not optimal.  Another alternative was to select a different location for the station.  
However, the north end of Laurel Road offers a unique geographic advantage of being adjacent to the 
source of supply and the takeaway capacity of the adjacent distribution system, making this location 
ideal from a gas system perspective. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
The current regulator station configuration is an obsolete design and poses a potential system 
reliability risk such as a Large Scale Customer Outage due to the obsolete design. 
 
 
Risk 2 
The current regulator station configuration and residential neighborhood location is not optimal and 
poses a potential system safety risk such as a Gas Distribution Event or Gas System Fire/Explosion 
event.  
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Greater system reliability 
• 
Reduced risk of a gas-related incident 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
Financial benefits include reduced station maintenance costs and any potential costs associated with 
reduced reliability and risk of a gas-related incident. 
 
3. Total cost 
The total cost of the project is estimated to be $602,500. 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
The estimated cost is based on costs of similar projects. 
 
5. Conclusion

from a gas incident, this project should be done.

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Upgrade Middletown Plant Yard Regulator Station 
Project/Program Manager: Michael Turanchik 
Project/Program Number (Level 1):  
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________

(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company will upgrade the station overpressure protection to include working regulators, monitor 
regulators, and relief valves, along with full redundancy and telemetric monitoring per our current 
design standards. 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
The current regulator station at the Middletown Plant Yard feeds the central area of Middletown and is 
of obsolete design. The regulator station was originally built in the early 1960s and was partially 
revamped in the 1980s.  It consists of working regulators and relief valves.  This project will upgrade 
the station overpressure protection to include working regulators, monitor regulators, and relief 
valves, along with full redundancy and telemetric monitoring, per current design standards.  This 
upgrade will add additional reliability and safety to the station. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Upgrading this regulator station will support the risk management goals of reliable distribution of gas 
and reduced risk of a gas-related incident. 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 


--- Page 137 ---

Alternatives 
 
An alternative was to select a different location for the station and build a new station.  This alternative 
was not selected because this station is ideally suited in its current location due to the unique 
geographic advantage of being adjacent to the source of supply and the takeaway capacity of the 
adjacent distribution system, making this location ideal from a gas system perspective. 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
The current regulator station configuration is an obsolete design and poses a potential system 
reliability risk such as a Large Scale Customer Outage due to the obsolete design. 
 
Risk 2 
The current regulator station configuration and residential neighborhood location is not optimal and 
poses a potential system safety risk such as a Gas Distribution Event or Gas System Fire/Explosion 
event.  
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Greater system reliability 
• 
Reduced risk of a gas-related incident

Financial benefits include reduced station maintenance costs and any potential costs associated with 
reduced reliability and risk of a gas-related incident. 
 
3. Total cost 
The total cost of the project is estimated to be $400,000. 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
The estimated cost is based on costs of similar projects.

Based on the greater reliability of an upgraded regulator station and the associated reduction of risk 
from a gas incident, this project should be done.

 2022-2024 
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☒ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title:  GCC Facility Upgrades - New Gas Control Center and LAN Room 
Project/Program Manager: Kenneth Fulton 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 2023 
CC. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $1,750 
O&M:  
 
DD. 
 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
EE. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
FF. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
This project is for a total renovation and relocation of the Gas Control Center (“GCC”). [Begin Redaction 
 
 
 
 
                                                                                                                                                          End Redaction]  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
This project provides an opportunity to strengthen and fortify the physical security of the GCC and 
address site vulnerabilities for this Tier 1 facility while upgrading the GCC whose facilities are 
approaching their end of life.  Increasing the physical security of the GCC will further align the Company 
with corporate and industry standards.  The project will also support FERC compliance relating to 
security of transmission system information. 
Additionally, equipment in the room has reached end-of-life and is no longer ergonomically correct.  
The new Control Center would have more ergonomically correct chairs and sit/stand desks for the 


--- Page 141 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 74 OF 141 
 
 
 
74 
Operators.  Replacing the Operators' consoles will allow the Company to address and mitigate fatigue 
related risks defined in the Fatigue Mitigation Section of Control Room Management.  (Department of 
Transportation/Pipeline and Hazardous Materials Safety Administration 49 CFR 192 Final Rule).   
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
n/a 
 
 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
The alternative is for GCC staff to continue to work out of the existing footprint and share the 
workspace with another group that is not Tier 1. This alternative was rejected due to the following: 
• 
Physical security risk allowing multiple groups access to the room. 
• 
Potential FERC violations in the event a marketer function employee sees transmission-related 
information.   
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
The alternative is for GCC staff to continue to work at the same location but to increase physical 
security measures by building a new room separating the Gas Control Center from the Gas Emergency 
Response Center (“GERC”).  A fixed post security guard to align with Electric and card access readers 
at the doors will restrict access to only Gas Control and IT Support staff personnel mitigating any 
FERC related issues with whom can see Transmission Function related information. This alternative 
was rejected due to the following: 
• 
The Company has outgrown the footprint with the increase in personnel (20%) over the last 10 
years.   
• 
COVID-19 social distancing measures would require additional changes to the room. 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
Risk 1  
 
Allowing non-Tier 1 personnel to have physical access to the GCC presents a risk to the Company as 
there is potential for catastrophic incorrect operation or malicious operation of the Company’s Gas 
Transmission and Distribution systems and Control Networks by unauthorized personnel.  This could 
lead to the possibilities of widespread customer outages, over-pressurizations, or other safety-related 
occurrences.  The current practice to mitigate this risk of unauthorized access is administrative.  Access 
is restricted to personnel whose core work function requires access to the GCC and the GERC that 


--- Page 142 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 75 OF 141 
 
 
 
75 
shares the room.  Visitors are required to be escorted in after the reason for their access is verified and 
their entry and egress are documented via a logbook. Having a separate secured Gas Control room will 
minimize the physical access of non-Tier 1 personnel to the GCC and will support the mitigation of 
ERMs such as Gas Transmission Event and Gas Distribution Event as well as the Orange and Rockland 
Enterprise Risks such as Large Scale Customer Outage and Gas System Fire/Explosion.  
 
 
Risk 2 
 
Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (“SCADA”) equipment (displays, turrets, etc.) and associated 
operator consoles and workstations have become dated. Outdated and antiquated hardware do not 
present optimal and industry accepted practices to best mitigate risk. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Increased safety and reliability 
• 
Compliance with data security 
• 
Improved workstreams and communications 
 
 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
n/a 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
 
This program could reduce O&M costs by working to better optimize command and control systems. 
This reduces the burden of communication and potential miscommunication, allowing for more reliable 
dispatching of personnel both on and off-hours, reducing time spent on overtime or on interrupted work. 
 
 
3. Total cost 
 
$1,750,000 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
 
The estimate includes a study from an outside architectural firm as well as an estimate from Corporate 
information Technology Group.  The project was approved in Con Edison’s current rate case for 
$6,000,000 for rate years 2021 – 2022 but was delayed due to COVID.  Orange and Rockland shares the 
Gas Control Center with Con Edison and shares costs at a split determined by the percentage of RTU’s 
in the field each company has.  The current split for the group is 80% Con Edison, 20% Orange and 
Rockland.  With estimates for the new room at $10,000,000 Orange and Rockland would be accountable 
for $2,000,000.  Of the $2,000,000 Orange and Rockland Gas Control has budgeted $125,000 for 2022 
and 2023 bringing the incremental rate case request to $1,750,000 or $875,000 for years 2022 and 2023. 
 
 
5. Conclusion 
 


--- Page 143 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 76 OF 141 
 
 
 
76 
This project provides an opportunity to strengthen and fortify the physical security of the GCC and 
address site vulnerabilities for this Tier 1 facility while upgrading the GCC whose facilities are 
approaching their end of life.

 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Gas Operations SCADA System Upgrade 
Project/Program Manager: Narriman Nasseri 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2022 
Estimated Date In Service:  2024 
EE. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $1,000.3  
O&M:  
 
FF.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000)

GG. 
5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense 
($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
HH. 
Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
Con Edison and Orange and Rockland Gas Operations use the Gas Operations Supervisory System 
(“GOSS”) to monitor and control the gas transmission and distribution systems.  GOSS is a Supervisory 
Control and Data Acquisition (“SCADA”) system that primarily supports Gas Control activities from 
the Van Nest Gas Control Center.  GOSS is used to monitor and control the Con Edison and Orange and 
Rockland Gas Transmission and Distribution system providing service to customers in Manhattan, 
northern Queens, the Bronx, most of Westchester and large portions of Orange and Rockland counties.  It 
monitors pressure gauge posts, and meters at the electric generating stations. It also monitors and 
controls regulators, gate stations, gas heating facilities, and remote operated valves (“ROVs”). 
This project will upgrade the GOSS hardware and software.  The current GOSS servers’ operating 
systems are on Windows 2012 and need to be upgraded to Windows 2016 or newer version.  The 
control workstations in the primary and the backup control center are on Windows 7 and need to be 
upgraded to Windows 10 or newer version.  With this upgrade, new hardware and software purchases 
are required.  The GOSS application is supported by the Aveva OASyS SCADA vendor.  This project 
will require labor from the vendor to perform the upgrade. 
 
Note: All funding for this project has been delayed to 2024 by CECONY. Orange and Rockland was 
made aware of this change in the schedule for the project spending after all the numbers were already 
submitted to Financial Services.    
 
Justification Summary: 
GOSS is a mission critical system required to support gas operations monitoring and control functions 
on a 24/7/365 basis.  The current system has been in operation since 2015.  For the system to continue 


--- Page 147 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 80 OF 141 
 
 
 
80 
to be reliable, available and have the latest cyber security measures in place, both the front-end 
workstation operating system as well as the back-end infrastructure must be updated to the most 
current software, hardware and licenses.   
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
n/a 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
There are no adequate alternative solutions due to the support and equipment requirements for the 
existing system.  The Company will no longer receive adequate vendor support on the outdated 
system/platform.  In addition, the current system will not be able to support the planned display 
update which will make it align with API 1165.  
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
 
Lack of security updates from Microsoft will make the current system out of compliance with cyber 
security mandates.  Cybersecurity has been identified as one of the Orange and Rockland Enterprise 
Risks.  
 
 
Risk 2 
 
Reduced ability of Aveva OASyS SCADA to provide support on outdated environment.  The system 
will be at cyber security risk. 
 
 
Risk 3 
 
End of life on hardware support. 
 
 
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits

4. Basis for estimate

5. Conclusion 
The system must be upgraded to address the above-mentioned risks and requirements.  Not 
proceeding with this upgrade project will increase the risks for hardware failure, unsupported system 
and being out of compliance with mandatory back-end infrastructure and cyber security requirements.

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Gas Outage Management System (OMS) 
Project/Program Manager:  Michelle Gionta 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 2024 
GG. 
Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $3,000.2 
O&M:  
 
HH. 
 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: $ 
Capital: 
II. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: $ 
Capital: 
JJ. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
A Gas Outage Management System (“OMS”) will provide an electronic/mobile solution to manage 
customer outage information in the event of a large-scale outage in order to better manage resources, 
decrease restoration time, and communicate in a timely manner to our customers and other 
stakeholders.  This will replace the current process, which is paper-based and manual.   
 
The plan is to develop specifications and requirements and issue a Request for Proposal (“RFP”) to 
develop the system.  The key elements the system should have: 
 
• 
Outage Detection:  The system would be able to identify incoming “No gas” calls across a 
widespread area to readily (visually) determine the impacted area and initiate quicker 
remedial actions.  
 
• 
Outage Management: After an area has been identified and the customers impacted have been 
determined, the system will control management of restoration information (e.g., turn-offs, 
turn-ons, inactive meters, etc.) of each customer, zone or region.  Field information will need to 
be recorded electronically in the field (hand-held device) by Company and Mutual Aid crews, 
which will be uploaded and managed accordingly to provide timely and accurate outage 
progress and reporting.   
 
• 
NRG Mapping integration: A Gas OMS system should integrate outage information with 
company’s NRG GIS based mapping system. 
 
• 
Gas Meter Advanced Metering Infrastructure (AMI) information: The Gas OMS system should 
be able to use AMI data to identify and verify customer status. 


--- Page 152 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 85 OF 141 
 
 
 
85 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Large Scale Gas Customer Outage risk was added to the Con Edison Inc. risk profile in the fourth 
quarter of 2019 and then to Orange and Rockland risk profile in the second quarter of 2020. A future 
mitigation control noted within the risk profile is to pursue a review the installation of a Gas OMS 
system.  This risk outlook is currently identified as increasing due to: 
 
• 
Substantial growth in customer demand and limited growth in supply-side resources;   
• 
Increased competition for procuring pipeline capacity controlled by other entities (“delivered 
services”) has reduced supply margins and results in less flexibility to circumvent 
operationally the effects of an upstream or on-system supply failure; and  
• 
There is less spare capacity under design-day or near design-day weather conditions. Recent 
events have raised concern regarding upstream gas pipeline integrity and operational 
flexibility. 
 
The Planning function in the gas Incident Command Structure (“ICS”) requires estimated time to 
restoration (“ETR”) in the event of an outage.  Having a Gas OMS system that can electronically record 
and transmit outage information will provide accurate information to manage the outage and provide 
timely information to all stakeholders, ultimately reducing restoration time which can be critical if the 
outage were to occur in below freezing temperatures where customers are displaced.  In addition, it 
would remove the manual time-consuming process which provides far less accurate information. 
 
Over the past three to four years there have been two major industry events which resulted in large 
scale gas customer outages (the 2018 Columbia Gas Event in Massachusetts, and the 2019 National 
Grid Aquidneck Island Outage in Rhode Island), where over 7,000 gas customers lost service in each 
event.  In the Rhode Island event, the Public Utilities Commission (PUC) recommended that the utility 
“create an outage mapping and tracking process” to better identify and track gas outages mainly due 
to the paper-based process used during the event. National Grid staffed personnel 24/7 for seven days 
to process outage/restoration cards to perform outage reporting and analysis for ~7,500 customers.  
 
In the event the outage is caused by the Company (e.g., operator error), there is a potential the 
Company will be responsible for some or all of the restoration costs. Reducing the likelihood of a large-
scale event and reducing restoration time would mitigate such risks. 
 
The recent increase in customer demand, denials of new pipelines into the northeast, and industry 
events have raised concerns regarding the ability to meet firm customer demand on a near peak 
demand day. Upstream pipeline events may reduce supply into the Company’s system and require the 
curtailment of firm gas customers. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Large Scale Gas Customer Outage risk was added to the Con Edison Inc. risk profile in the fourth 
quarter of 2019 and then to Orange and Rockland risk profile in the second quarter of 2020. A future 
mitigation control was identified for this risk.

5. Conclusion 
 
 
Project Risks and Mitigation Plan 
 
Risk 1                                                                        Mitigation plan 
Gas OMS is a new technology category therefore it is not commonly used in the industry.  Mitigation 
will be multi-stepped. 
 
• 
Leverage the Company’s knowledge from its electric outage management system, and gas 
mobile dispatch system used over the last 15 years  
 
• 
Seek a solution compatible with the Company’s current Electric OMS system. 
 
• 
Benchmark industry groups such as Northeast Gas Association (“NGA”) and other utilities to 
understand and participate in the scope of needs in this space. 
 
• 
Use performance-based contracting where possible.  
 
 
Risk 2                                                                        Mitigation plan 
Large outages are not common so unfamiliarity with system could be an issue for users initially and 
over time if the system is not used often.  This risk can be mitigated by using the system during normal 
business (smaller outages) and conducting regular and outline mandated training/drills in the Gas 
Emergency Response Plan (ERP).

of field data electronically via GMD and upload to a Gas OMS system would be required.  (e.g., Gas 
Mobile Phone/tablet app to record meter number and status (e.g., off and locked, or turned on, 
currently locked/inactive) 
 
Using AMI gas meter data to assist in determining field status could be beneficial. 
 
NRG Mapping system – using the current GIS based mapping system to visually display outage 
information could be beneficial

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Gas Mobile Dispatch Replacement Project 
Project/Program Manager: Michelle Gionta / 
Janice Nichols 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 22094341 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☒ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________

Estimated Date In Service: June 30, 2022 
II. Total Funding Request ($000)

☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000)

vendor support

LL. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
A new vendor solution for dispatching field work to gas personnel will afford the Company a means 
to continue supporting its critical business functions of responding to gas emergencies and complying 
with New York Public Service Commission mandated gas inspections. A new solution will allow for 
the replacement of the obsolete WorkUP v.6.9.74.14, the software for the Company’s Gas Mobile 
Dispatch (GMD) system. WorkUP is vendor software. The vendor has informed the Company that 
they are no longer upgrading the software. Over time, WorkUP will not run as reliably as it does 
today, since the database, middleware, servers, wireless communications, and other interfacing 
technology will continue to be enhanced and become out of sync with WorkUP. The Company is 
already starting to see some degradation of the environment since the software uses an older version of 
Oracle.   
 
The current GMD system, originally installed in 2005, is deemed a high criticality business system. When 
implemented, it replaced paper-based procedures. The system is used daily (24x7) by the Company’s 
Gas Emergency Response Center (GERC) dispatchers and Gas Operations personnel. The system is used 
to dispatch gas emergency work and mandated inspection work for valves, buildings of public assembly, 
red tag violation re-inspections, surveillance and repairs to outside gas leaks, gate/district regulator 
station inspections, meter orders, and multi-meter regulators. Field personnel enter data in the system 
using mobile data terminals that are installed in Company vehicles. GMD interfaces with the Company’s 
Trouble Order System for gas emergency trouble calls, Customer Information Management System 
(CIMS) for meter order work, the Gas Inspection and Maintenance System (GIMS) for the mandated 
inspection work, and the Work Management System (WMS) for follow-up work.  
 
The scope of this project is to replace the GMD system with VxField solution. The solution is architected 
in a current technology platform and fully supported by the vendor, Accruent. Improvements over 


--- Page 158 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 91 OF 141 
 
 
 
91 
WorkUP are expected from this new implementation. They include streamlining data entry in templates 
used by field personnel, simplifying the manner in which jobs are assigned/reassigned to field 
personnel, reducing duration of scheduled maintenance and system downtime, and the inclusion of new 
templates to capture Gas Engineering compliance information that is currently stored in a separate 
environment. 
 
This project will consist of system design, development, and implementation. The design will include a 
review of business requirements, development of functional requirements and design, technical review 
for integration with Con Edison Gas Central Exadata platform, software/hardware requirements, and 
implementation plan. The implementation will include the development of customized templates, 
configurations, system interfaces, wireless connectivity, other hardware/software upgrades, and end-
user training.  
 
Timeline: 
 
   
 
Justification Summary: 
 
The GMD system is heavily relied upon to aid Gas Operations in meeting corporate and regulatory goals 
regarding gas safety and reliability, including responding to gas odor calls in a timely manner. Further, 
the GMD system aids in scheduling, tracking, and the data collection of multiple mandated inspections, 
such as valves, buildings of public assembly, red tag violation re-inspections, surveillance and repairs to 
outside gas leaks, gate/district regulator station inspections, meter orders, and multi-meter regulators. 
The system provides a means for capturing consistent and complete data from the field through 
customized templates.   
 
While the WorkUP vendor is still supporting the version of the product that the Company utilizes, it 
has notified the Company that they will no longer upgrade the software. Over time, as database, 
middleware, communications, and hardware are upgraded, it is expected that the WorkUP software 
will not be as reliable as it is today. For example, the WorkUP software runs on an application server 
that uses Oracle client 11g. Oracle support for version 11g ends December 2020. The vendor of 
WorkUP researched recompiling their code and upgrading to a newer version of Oracle client, yet they 
would not certify that the environment would be stable. The Company will isolate the application 


--- Page 159 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 92 OF 141 
 
 
 
92 
server from the network and to lock it down to specific users by ring fencing the server. This is a 
workaround and not a permanent long-term solution. 
 
WorkUP was acquired by its current vendor in September 2016 and currently only two other companies 
besides Con Edison/O&R are using WorkUP. Con Edison Gas Operations is currently replacing all their 
gas related work management systems, which includes WorkUP, with vendor CGI Asset and Resource 
Management (ARM) solution.  
 
Investing in this system replacement provides sustained reliability on a mobile platform for Gas 
Operations to address critical core business tasks and compliance requirements that need to be 
accomplished daily. This platform will also make it easier for the Company to enhance its GMD system 
to respond to unknown future business and/or regulatory requirements. Some immediate benefits 
related to this project include: 
 
Improved Field Employee Performance 
• 
The use of new, more user-friendly templates for field personnel will be much more flexible than 
those currently in use, allowing personnel to better capture information they observe in the field  
  
Improved Business Productivity 
• 
This involves adding functionality to the new solution to capture Gas Engineering compliance 
information. Currently this information is captured on paper and keyed into a SharePoint site. 
Incorporating the collection of this information into the system through the field templates will allow 
for a consolidated gas distribution repository and provide opportunities for comprehensive data 
analytics. The information contains, but is not limited to, external corrosion, mechanical fitting 
failures, plastic pipe failures, and general job orders.  
• 
This new solution will make it easier to recall a job that may have been sent to a specific machine 
and needs to be reassigned. This recall feature will simplify the dispatch process and eliminate the 
need for manual workarounds by the System Administrators.   
 
Improved IT Productivity 
• 
The maintenance of templates performed by programmers will become less cumbersome in the new 
solution. In WorkUP, several templates contain the same information, so a change to a field or 
addition of a field to one template necessitates changes to other templates. In the new solution, 
information on templates will be streamlined and the solution will be designed to provide System 
Administrators with the ability to control template changes through configuration settings.   
• 
The new solution will have improved architecture that will reduce the downtime associated with 
system maintenance activities.  
 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
This is a mission critical system that handles some of Gas Operations' most important work and cannot 
be compromised in any way.  
Departmental risks:  
 
Department Gas Operations Operating Errors 
The current software is used for dispatching gas emergency work and mandated inspection work for 
valves, buildings of public assembly, red tag violation re-inspections, surveillance and repairs to 
outside gas leaks, gate/district regulator station inspections, meter orders, and multi-meter

EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 93 OF 141 
 
 
 
93 
regulators. The software has reached end-of-life and is no longer being upgraded by the vendor. Over 
time, the software will not run as reliably as it does today, which could lead to loss of workforce 
productivity and operating errors. 
 
Department Information Technology Unsupported technology in computer systems 
The current software is used for dispatching gas emergency work and mandated inspection work. The 
software is at end-of-life and vendor has stated they will not be making any upgrades to the software. 
Without upgrades the reliability of the software will deteriorate. As the vendor moves their customers 
to other solutions (only two companies remain with the current software) the cost of maintenance and 
support continues to rise. The increase from 2019 to 2020 was almost 10%. It went from $66.8K to 
$73.6K. 
 
Department Information Technology Failure of critical business application 
The current software is used for dispatching gas emergency work and mandated inspection work. The 
vendor has stated they will not be making any upgrades to the software. Without upgrades, the 
software won't run as reliably as it does today since database, middleware, servers, wireless 
communications, and other interfacing technology will continue to be enhanced and become out of 
sync with the current technology. 
 
Department Gas Operations Regulatory Non-Compliance 
The current software is used for dispatching gas emergency work and mandated inspection work. As 
the software reliability deteriorates, PSC audit findings could increase, causing penalties to be 
incurred. Any compromise to the reliability of the system could result in delays or lapses. Penalties 
could arise from events caused by delays or lapses, or from such things as missed inspections.    
 
Department Gas Operations Safety 
Because of the critical nature of the work processed through GMD, any compromise to the reliability of 
the system, particularly as it relates to emergency work, could impact public safety. For example, odor 
calls not processed correctly could result in delayed response which directly impacts public safety.   
 
Department Gas Operations Gas Distribution Event 
Because of the critical nature of the work processed through GMD, any compromise to the reliability of 
the system, particularly as it relates to emergency work, could lead to a distribution event. For 
example, odor calls not processed correctly could result in delayed response when every second 
counts.

Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
CGI Asset and Resource Management (ARM) was rejected because upon the Company reviewing this

in the same instance as Con Edison. The solution required modifications, for example, the addition of 
company code, to differentiate between companies. ARM, as designed today, does not include 
functionality for substations, for the Company to utilize ARM for their entire work management, 
substation functionality would need to be added to ARM.

Converting back to a paper-based practice, which was the environment prior to the WorkUP 
implementation in 2005, is not an option. It increases the risk of not meeting mandated corporate and 
regulatory goals.

Project Risks and Mitigation Plan 
 
Risk 1 Capital projects, such as the CORE, could impact some deliverables due to internal resource 
constraints. 
Mitigation plan Where possible, factor in the timing of CORE development work into this project 
schedule, extend the testing timeframe and field training schedule to alleviate strain on business 
resources, and hiring outside resources to supplement internal IT resources.   
 
Technical Evaluation / Analysis 
 
The Company performed high level fit/gap analysis of the in-house solution currently used by O&R’s 
Customer Meter Operations, Field Order (FORD). This solution is tightly integrated with the Customer 
Information Management System, and because of that, it would require a complete redesign of the 
database in order to support the needs of the gas related templates.  
 
The Company conducted a design study with vendor CGI to determine the feasibility of moving onto 
their Asset and Resource Management (ARM) solution since Con Edison Gas Operations is in the 
process of installing the solution and Con Edison Electric Operations has been using the solution for 
several years. During the study, the ARM scheduling and mobility modules were analyzed for the 
Work Up functionality as well as for potential work management benefits in the future. There are some 
significant reasons as to why the Company cannot utilize ARM or fully realize benefits from the Con 
Edison installations. ARM does not have a feature across all their products to differentiate between the 
two Companies. Adding this feature, for example, a company code, requires significant ARM 
customization. Additionally, Con Edison’s installation splits electric and gas operating areas into two 
different environments. This does not meet O&R’s need to standardize processes across all operating 
areas. ARM does not have substation functionality, if O&R were to see benefits in using ARM, it would 
need to contain functionality for all three operating areas and that would be significant customization. 
The design study with CGI was halted when Con Edison had performance issues with CGI. The 3-
month effort for O&R with CGI was priced at $696,900 + $65,000 expenses. The VxField design and 
installation cost is $453,600. VxField offers all functionality that O&R needs and is a much more 
straightforward installation. O&R will be able to utilize Con Edison’s Oracle database servers which 
lessens the data center footprint for O&R and provides some cost savings.

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: LocusView 
Project/Program Manager: Daniel Alves 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☒ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 1/1/2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 12/31/2024 
KK. 
Total Funding Request ($951.9)  
Capital: 15% 
O&M: 85% 
 
LL.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($363) 
Capital: 363 
MM. 
5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense 
($1544) 
O&M: $1.3M 
Capital: $244K 
NN. 
Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
Orange and Rockland Utilities has been working with LocusView Solutions since 2015 to geospatially 
record new asset locations and fusion records. Gas Engineering, Contract Administration Group 
(“CAG”), Mapping, IT Business Systems Delivery, and Information Technology (“IT”) (cyber security) 
have been working closely with LocusView to tailor and develop the LocusView application to meet 
Orange and Rockland's needs. 
 
Due to the proven capabilities and benefits of LocusView to Orange & Rockland over the last few 
years, Gas Engineering and Operations proposes to fully implement this technology. Accurate gas 
maps are the foundation of a safe gas system. Because gas facilities are buried, accurate maps are 
critical to safely operate and maintain the gas system. Implementing a new electronic mapping system 
and the associated processes is a significant step change in the Gas organization. LocusView will create 
electronic as-builts, that comply with Gas Supervisor’s Operating Manual (“GSOM”) 601 and integrate 
the GPS data with the Company’s NRG mapping system to form a searchable database.  
 
To support full implementation of LocusView, the Company will require new and replacement 
LocusView units (e.g., GPS, Tablet, Scanner), licenses for both Field and Web users, and internal 
technical support from both Operational System Support (“OSS”) and IT. 
 
Justification Summary: 
With LocusView fully implemented the following benefits will be realized, justifying the need for this 
project:
 
• 
Reduced excavation damages enabled by accurate GPS locations of assets. 
• 
Current process of producing paper as-builts will be replaced with electronic as-builts using 
GPS accurate data.   


--- Page 166 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 99 OF 141 
 
 
 
99 
• 
Real time verification of operator qualifications.   
• 
Improved efficiency for determining valve locations in an emergency response situation.   
• 
Enhanced knowledge of the system supporting a stronger Distribution Integrity Management 
Program and Pipeline Safety Management System (American Petroleum Institute 1173).   
• 
Searchable database of material information.   
• 
Compliance with future state and federal regulations on tracking and traceability of fusion 
and gas assets. 
• 
Future cost savings from enhanced damage prevention. 
LocusView began as a subsidiary of the Gas Technology Institute and Operations Technology 
Development and through this research has been specifically made for the gas utility 
industry.  Overall, there are a limited number of commercially available software/hardware 
applications that capture a combination of fusion, GPS, construction data, and material property 
data.  LocusView provides the capability to capture all this information in one application. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
Having access to sub-inch GPS accurate locations of assets will lead to better locating and a reduction 
of excavation damages, therefore mitigating the risk of fire/explosion.   
 
 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
Control Point and Fuse to Fuser were considered for operator qualification verification. However, 
neither program offered the ability to map all assets. 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
  
The Company could leave the process the way it is (paper-based and manual) which would not 
capture the additional information nor provide the benefits of LocusView. Internal NRG SME's have 
indicated NRG is not capable of providing a product similar to LocusView. The intent is to leverage 
existing systems, but it was deemed not feasible.  
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
Distribution Event - Currently, assets and fusions must be located from fusion logs and hand drawn 
as-builts which identifies the location of the assets and fuses but it is still a manual and  time 


--- Page 167 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 100 OF 141 
 
 
 
100 
consuming process. A searchable database of material/fusion information will greatly enhance the 
speed and precision of tracking and locating these assets and fusions.  This is critical in an emergency. 
 
 
Risk 2 
Regulatory Risk -There is a future risk of federal and/or state regulations relating to tracking and 
traceability programs.  
 
Risk 3 
Financial Risk – Manufacture recall or defective equipment recall.  In the past the Company had to 
perform excavations to determine locations of defective equipment.  Not having accurate GIS data led 
to additional excavation cost since exact locations were not available.  If the Company captures all the 
information that LocusView provides, it will allow quicker and more precise field location of these 
assets for remediation.   
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
• 
Operational Excellence – Implementing this technology positions the gas business to have 
more capability to leverage GIS data to better manage the gas system.  
• 
Enhanced system knowledge supporting a stronger Distribution Integrity Management 
Program and Pipeline Safety Management System (API 1173).   
• 
Compliance with potential federal/state regulations on tracking and traceability of fusion and 
gas assets. 
 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
N/A 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
 
One of the major benefits of the implementation of this project will include an estimated cost avoidance 
of $363,000 over the next three years for the replacement of hand drawn as-builts, and manually 
created fusion logs done daily by field inspectors. 
 
3. Total cost 
 
$951,900 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
Estimate is based on historical experience with Locus View through the Company’s R&D program as 
well as previous estimates and current implementation cost. 
 
5. Conclusion 
 
This project should be completed because implementing a new electronic data capturing system and 
the associated process improvements is a significant step forward toward achieving operational 
excellence. It also enables the use of newly collected data which will help improve safety and efficiency 
when performing task related to locating buried facilities.

Project/Program Title: Driver Training 
Project/Program Manager: Jackie Winter 
Project/Program Number (Level 1):

Work Description: 
 
Orange and Rockland is planning on hiring a vendor to help it achieve the objective of the Driving 
Training Program to enhance safety through safer driving and to avoid costly motor vehicle collisions.  
The vendor will assess the current state of the program, analyze historical performance and accident 
root causes, and assist in continuous improvement.  The Program involves two years providing 
analysis and training to the Company trainers, and one year of analysis and comprehensive, hands-on 
driver training with the entire Gas Operations workforce.  The program will likely take place during 
the construction season timeframe with seasonal/situational tips included as part of the training.  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Gas Operations has worked to improve driving safety for its workforce in order to reduce preventable 
motor vehicle accidents.  The Company has experience with key vendors that are dedicated to 
delivering customized training specifically for the purpose of helping companies drive more safely.  
While the driver program is maturing, the continued assistance of a dedicated trainer will help Gas 
Operations reach its safety goals.  Motor vehicle accidents are costly to the Company and its 
stakeholders, and this program can pay significant dividends in the areas of safety and cost avoidance. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
The program is aligned with corporate initiatives and reducing enterprise risks.  With respect to Safety, 
the program is intended to continue to improve driving safety performance which is a part of the 
Company’s overall safety program.  Reducing accidents supports cost containment initiatives.   


--- Page 171 ---

2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could utilize internal resources to advance the program from its current state to the 
desired state.  The reason for rejecting this approach is that focused vendors are more efficient and 
better equipped to deliver a customized driving safety curriculum.  Expertise in this area is held by 
vendors who specialize in safe driving skills and understand and teach industry and non-industry best 
practices.  Utilizing in-house personnel would not provide the same value as a specialized vendor. 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection

Risk 1

financially catastrophic or personally tragic accident.  
 
 
Risk 2 
 
If safety practices do not improve, the Company will continue to incur accident related costs at an 
unacceptable rate.   
 
 
Risk 3 
 
Every preventable accident risks the Company’s image in the community and subjects the community 
to avoidable safety hazards. 
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Improved safety of employees and members of the communities served by the Company.

to do on the roadways when confronted with a dangerous situation.

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☒ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Inside Service Inspections  
Project/Program Manager: Michael Turanchik 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☒ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:1/1/2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 
OO. 
Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $600 
 
PP.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
QQ. 
5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense 
($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
RR. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
This program expands upon the current inside gas service inspection program to facilitate additional 
actions associated with resolving chronic “can’t get in” issues, where certain customers make it very 
challenging for the Company to complete the inspections on an ongoing basis.  The Company has 
found a number of instances where we were able to complete the baseline inside service inspections, 
but were challenged to complete subsequent periodic inspections.  The additional actions for this 
program would include the relocation of gas meter sets outdoors, possible service terminations, and all 
the administration associated with these follow-up actions. 
 
Justification Summary: 
As part of the April 20, 2017 Commission Order in Case 15-G-0244 (“2017 Order”), the Company was 
required to comply with new requirements associated with the change of definition of a gas service 
line, which required the leak survey and atmospheric corrosion inspections of indoor gas meter sets.  
Despite best efforts by the Company to communicate with customers regarding the importance of 
timely inside service inspections, a number of customers have made it very difficult for the Company 
to complete these on an ongoing basis.  This program would provide funding for the Company to 
either move the gas meter sets outdoors or otherwise provide gas service terminations by cutting and 
capping the gas service pipes, along with the associated administration of this program.  
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
This program supports the management of regulatory risk and safety risk associated with the 
challenges of performing inside service inspections on an ongoing basis.  
 
 
 


--- Page 176 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 109 OF 141 
 
 
 
109 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
Make additional inspection attempts and process financial bill penalties for customers who continue to 
deny access to perform ongoing inside gas service inspections.  This was rejected because it is believed 
that additional inspection attempts and penalties would be resource intensive and present additional 
unnecessary regulatory and safety risk. 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
There is a risk of non-compliance with the 2017 Order on an ongoing basis.  The Company has already 
been cited in the Department of Public Service  2020 record audit as an “area of concern” for the inside 
services that had not been inspected.  Further non-compliances could result in Commissionpenalties. 
 
Risk 2 
This is also a safety risk for not being able to inspect inside gas service piping on an ongoing basis.  
Concerns include gas leaks, corrosion, and non-compliant pipe and fittings, which could result in gas 
incidents. 
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Regulatory compliance with the 2017 Order 
• 
Customer Safety 
• 
Identification of theft of service condition 
• 
Improved customer satisfaction with having a safer installation and having an outdoor gas meter 
set which avoids the inconvenience of the Company needing to enter their premises on a regular 
basis. 
 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
N/A 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
N/A 
 
3. Total cost 
$600,000 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
It is estimated that approximately 1% of customers with inside gas meters (about 120 total) would 
require their gas service to be moved outdoors or terminated, on an ongoing basis.  The cost per service 
is estimated to be approximately $5,000 each, including administration, costing $200,000 per year for 
RY1, RY2 and RY3.  
 
5. Conclusion 


--- Page 177 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 110 OF 141 
 
 
 
110 
The program improves safety and helps facilitate the ongoing compliance with the2017 Order.

Risk 1                                                                        Mitigation plan 
Cost overrun                                                        Close monitoring of program

Not applicable

Work Description: 
This program is to hire contractors to perform pre-emergent spraying and a thorough gas right of way 
clearing of heavy vegetation to facilitate the performance of code-mandated leakage survey and 
patrols, close interval surveys, and related work.  
 
Justification Summary: 
Right of way clearing is required to be able to perform code-mandated tasks.  Additional work in Gas 
Technical Operations (including AMI meter follow-up, service regulator troubleshooting, telemetry 
support, and enhanced equipment inspections) has resulted in less available time for ROW clearing.  
This program is necessary to clear all of the gas right of ways to the point that they can be managed by 
Company crews in the future. Benefits include the avoidance of financial penalties assessed by the 
New York State Public Service Commission associated with non-compliance. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
This program supports compliance-based risks.

small tools. (e.g., handheld weed-trimmers)

Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title:    Gas Engineer, Technology Support 
Project/Program Manager: Debbie DiMeo 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 1/1/2023 
Estimated Date In Service: 
SS. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $253.8 
 
TT.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
UU. 
5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense 
($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
VV. 
Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
The Company plans to hire an engineering support position focusing on developing, implementing, 
and supporting various new and existing technologies and technical program areas. Activities would 
include advancing the use of a new Geographic Information System (LocusView) for accurate asset 
mapping, improving the accuracy and the ability to extract data from the NRG mapping system, 
performing data analysis using Gas Mobile Dispatch data collected from the Gas Incident Management 
System (“GIMS”) to enhance understanding of system risk, and supporting development of a GAS 
Outage Management System (“OMS”) that includes tracking outages and restoration progress.    
 
The support provided through this gas engineering function will serve to provide the additional 
mitigative actions and analysis that could reduce the risks of several Enterprise Risk Management 
(“ERMs”) (Gas System Fire/Explosion, Gas Transmission Event, Gas Distribution Event and Large 
Scale Gas Outage) as well as reduce threats to the system as captured in the Distribution Integrity 
Management Plan (“DIMP”). This position will also provide the support required to extract the data 
and perform the analysis to support decisions regarding operationally required activities such as 
making critical repairs on gas main and responding to gas emergencies. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
As the gas system grows more complex with the adoption of new technology, engineering support will 
be required to focus on developing, implementing, and supporting the various technologies.  
Currently, there are several key technical programs the Company is undertaking, which requires this 
support: 
 
1. LocusView is a GIS based mapping system that will track and record new gas assets being 
installed as well as existing assets (e.g., valves and mains operating at or over 124 psig). 


--- Page 184 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 117 OF 141 
 
 
 
117 
Mapping gas assets is a core/critical task and requires an engineer to ensure the software’s 
associated technical/business rules and integrity of the data are accurate. Technical support is 
also required for ensuring new updates are executed accurately.   
 
2. Gas Engineering is continuing efforts to update the Company’s NRG mapping system which 
will require field review validation (e.g. – sensor lines on regulators, no curb valve locations, 
LocusView accuracy). 
 
3. Gas Engineering oversees the Planning Section in the Incident Command System (“ICS”) 
structure of a gas outage.  This function, amongst other duties, is responsible for tracking 
outage/restoration progress and provide Estimated Time of Response (“ETRs”).  As the GAS 
OMS system is developed, support will be needed to ensure these elements are incorporated 
and maintained. Other intermediate solutions are being pursued prior to full Gas OMS being 
implemented.  
 
4. Orange and Rockland plans to install additional gas sensors in future years (e.g. – methane-
Natural Gas Detectors (“NGDs”), pressure, flow, valve box tampering) which will require an 
engineer to develop the technical/business needs plus support the sensor technology 
development, field installation and coordination with the Advanced Metering Infrastructure 
(“AMI”) team to leverage the AMI system. 
 
5. As the NY environmental laws (Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act) are 
implemented, Orange and Rockland will become more engaged in developing Non-Pipe 
Alternative (“NPA”) programs (e.g., Demand Response pilot) to reduce system peak demand 
and consumption. As new solutions are developed this engineer would help support the 
solicitation and review of NPA programs.  
 
The support provided through this gas engineering function serves to provide additional mitigative 
actions that reduces the risks of several Enterprise Risk Management (“ERMs”) (Gas System 
Fire/Explosion, Gas Transmission Event, Gas Distribution Event and Large Scale Gas Outage) as well 
as reduce threats to the system as captured in the Distribution Integrity Management Plan (“DIMP”).  
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Public and employee safety, operational excellence, customer experience, and cost management are 
key aspects of the Company’s long-range plan objective. All of the technologies and programs that this 
gas engineering position will be supporting are focused on public and employee safety and operational 
excellence. Additionally, through better data collection and communications, customer experience 
would also be enhanced. 
 
LocusView technology is aimed to a large extent at damage prevention. Access to accurate GPS 
locations will improve line locating accuracy and reduce damages. LocusView also provides the 
capability to electronically collect, store and track all pipe material, fittings, and fusion records. The 
system will collect and verify detailed information for fusers and peer inspectors, such as operator 
qualification records. Customer experience will be improved via Gas OMS and implementation of 
LocusView technology as outage areas may be reduced and responded to more quickly and with 
timely information for the customer. Data collection through GMD/GIMs and through new sensor 
technology will serve to reduce system risk by selecting areas, equipment, or material within the gas 
system to focus resources on to eliminate threats, thereby improving safety. 
 
 


--- Page 185 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1)

118 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
An alternative is to maintain status quo and use the existing resources (e.g., personnel and O&M 
budget) in the Gas Engineering Department for these activities. Unfortunately, there are not sufficient 
resources within Gas Engineering to perform this work to the degree merited. As data becomes more 
advanced, available, and plentiful, this new resource is critical in developing and refining the 
technologies, along with interpreting and utilizing the resulting data. 
 
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
Given current levels of resources, if no action is taken, Gas Engineering will not be able to provide 
proper support and attention to these technical programs the Company is undertaking. This will 
dramatically slow the ability to implement the new technologies and to use the crucial data that would 
have resulted in efficient ways to improve the Safety and Operations of the gas system. This could also 
ultimately jeopardize the successful completion of these programs.

5. Conclusion 
As the gas system grows more complex with the adoption of new technologies, dedicated engineering 
support will be required to focus on developing, implementing and supporting the various 
technologies outlined in this document. The Company believes this effort requires the attention and 
oversight of a new dedicated engineer to manage to ensure it is successful. As data becomes more 
advanced, available and plentiful, this new resource is critical in developing and refining the 
technologies, along with interpreting and utilizing the resulting data for the Company. 
 
The support provided through this gas engineering function serves to provide additional mitigative 
actions that reduces the risks of several ERMs (Gas System Fire/Explosion, Gas Transmission Event, 
Gas Distribution Event and Large Scale Gas Outage) as well as reduce threats to the system as captured 
in the Distribution Integrity Management Plan (DIMP).  
Project Risks and Mitigation Plan 
 
Risk 1                                                                        Mitigation plan 
Employing one individual will assist in moving some of the aforementioned technologies along in a 
positive and productive way for the Company. However, in order to bring all of the Company’s 
technologies and technical program areas to fruition is not reasonable with this one position. A risk is 
to spread the new position too thin thereby not achieving any solid improvements in any of the areas. 
To avoid this, the Company will ensure that expectations are managed upfront, and that focused 
objectives, plans and reasonable deliverables and achievements are clearly outlined. 
 
Risk 2                                                                        Mitigation plan

enhancements, AMI, non-pipe solutions technology, etc.
