Gas Operations and Engineering 
 2022-2024  
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Gas Engineer - PSMS (Pipeline Safety Management Systems) and Integrity 
Management/Data Analytic Support 
Project/Program Manager: Debra DiMeo 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2021 
Estimated Date In Service: On-going 
UU. 
Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $450 
 
VV. 
 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
WW. 
5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense 
($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
XX. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company plans to create a gas engineering technical specialist position that would assist in 
guiding and progressing the Orange and Rockland Pipeline Safety Management Systems (“PSMS”) 
program and the distribution and transmission integrity management programs.  Distribution and 
transmission integrity programs are mandated by federal and state regulations. PSMS is a strategic, 
industry accepted safety practice recommended by the U.S. National Transportation Safety Board 
(“NTSB”) and developed into American Petroleum Institute (“API”) Recommended Practice 1173 in 
partnership with the U.S. Pipeline and Hazardous Materials Safety Administration (“PHMSA”).  
The intention of this request is to establish a position that will support Orange and Rockland’s integrity 
and PSMS programs.  
• 
The Company plans to fill the position in 2021 
• 
Gap Analysis review which includes fact checking to verify both gaps and non-gaps to be 
completed in 2021  
• 
Concurrently, several of the more prominent known gaps will continue to be addressed – 2021 
and future 
• 
High Level Action Plan and general “road map” will be developed to cohesively plan to 
address deficiencies found in the Gap Analysis - 2022 
• 
Detailed Action Plan developed and implementation in 2023.  
 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
The gas industry is moving toward all gas utilities adopting a comprehensive PSMS for overall 
improved safety and the avoidance of incidents. API RP 1173 has become a standard industry practice 


--- Page 190 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 123 OF 141 
 
 
 
123 
and is supported and encouraged by the New York State Public Service Commission. The American 
Gas Association (“AGA”) has also made a formal recommendation that all gas utilities develop a 
PSMS.   
 
With the support of consultants, Orange and Rockland has started the initial phase (gap analysis) of 
developing a PSMS. Orange and Rockland Gas Engineering support is required to review the gap 
analysis and verify the results (both the gaps and the non-gaps) in order to determine a path forward 
to address all deficiencies in an Action Plan. A future “PSMS road map” must be created and 
implemented over the next 3-4 years for Orange and Rockland’s plan to be comprehensive and 
sustainable.  An engineer is needed to support the current engineering team and is important for 
retention of Company-based subject matter experts (“SME”) knowledge in house.  Department of 
Public Service staff, in the most recent rate case, encouraged Orange and Rockland to make a proposal 
to hire resources internally to support the continuity of these efforts and to retain SME knowledge.   
 
This position would also support the continued development and improvement of the DIMP and 
TIMP integrity management programs, while addressing new TIMP Mega Rule code requirements. In 
particular, a new integrity risk model is being developed that will use internal and external data 
sources and will require regular updates and maintenance.  Also, as new technology provides more 
data (e.g., Advanced Metering Infrastructure (“AMI”), LocusView, sensors) this engineer could help 
support the collection, analysis and reporting of new data to improve analysis, reporting and overall 
design and operational decision making. 
 
PSMS and integrity management programs are measures that mitigate the effects of escaping gas, gas 
explosions and/or gas burning out of control related to either distribution or transmission systems. 
Creation of the requested position will be another measure to support mitigation of Enterprise Risk 
Management (“ERMs”) such as Gas Transmission Event and Gas Distribution Event, as well as the 
Orange and Rockland Enterprise Risk, Gas System Fire/Explosion.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Public and employee safety is a key aspect of the Company’s long-range plan objective.  PSMS and the 
integrity programs support this objective.  PSMS is centered on the Plan-Do-Check-Act cycle to harness 
continuous safety improvements.  The central purpose of DIMP and TIMP are to enhance safety by 
identifying and reducing/minimizing pipeline safety risks.  Integrity programs are based on Plan-Do-
Check-Act cycles of continuous improvement as well.  
 
PSMS and integrity management programs are measures that mitigate the effects escaping gas, gas 
explosions and/or gas burning out of control related to either distribution or transmission systems.  
Creation of the requested position will be another measure to support mitigation of ERMs such as Gas 
Transmission Event and Gas Distribution Event, as well as the Orange and Rockland Enterprise Risk, 
Gas System Fire/Explosion.  
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
The Company could utilize consulting services for the function as described in this request.  This is a 
rejected option as it is critical to have an Orange and Rockland employee who is on the premises, 
interacts daily and is exposed to all Orange and Rockland departments, understands fully the culture 


--- Page 191 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 124 OF 141 
 
 
 
124 
of the Company, knows the people, and how Orange and Rockland is structured and functions to 
appropriately fill the position as described.   Orange and Rockland will use consulting services for 
other duties associated with PSMS and integrity when it is not critical that an Orange and Rockland 
employee perform the function.   Since PSMS targets safety culture changes, it is essential to have an 
Orange and Rockland employee in this role.  
 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
If a new position is not created, the ability to adequately address the gaps identified in the PSMS gap 
analysis, develop an action plan and “road map” to zero harm as envisioned in a PSMS, is in jeopardy.  
Sustaining a continuous improvement cycle for the integrity programs would be more difficult to 
achieve as well.  
 
 
Risk 2 
 
Risk 3 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
• 
Increased safety and reliability 
•

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☒ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title:  Gas Control – Trainer/Compliance Position 
Project/Program Manager: Kenneth Fulton 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 1/1/2024 
Estimated Date In Service: 6/1/2024 
WW. 
Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $125.0 
 
XX.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
YY. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
ZZ. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
As the amount of information and data received in Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition 
(“SCADA”) by Gas Control continues to increase, a position to oversee compliance with the Control 
Room Management Plan and with training (SCADA Simulator training, drills, and Table Top 
Exercises) the Gas System Operators is needed. This was a gap identified through peer benchmarking 
and would support compliance with the Control Room Management (“CRM”) Plan. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Maintaining compliance with PHMSA's CRM plan is critical. Increasing oversight of the plan coupled 
with frequent updating of the Plan while retaining documentation and records for 6-years requires a 
dedicated person for oversight.  With an increased focus on further training related to situational 
awareness and operator response to abnormal or emergency operating conditions, this employee 
would help to support proper corrective actions taken by the Gas System Operator. 
 
Learning from the Rhode Island Gas Outage of 2019, we have focused a lot of time and preparation in 
the event O&R experiences a similar event. One of the lessons learned the Company is taking from that 
event is to have a dedicated person to oversee the training program of the Operators to create scenarios 
in SCADA for Gas System Operators to increase their situational awareness in how to respond to a 
large-scale outage.   
 
Complacency with training by not having oversight of the SCADA simulator and dedication to the 
creation of more drills and/or table-top exercises for the Gas System Operators could lead to a 
potential operating error and Orange and Rockland Enterprise Risk such as a Large Scale Customer 
Outage. 


--- Page 195 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 128 OF 141 
 
 
 
128 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
Creating this position mitigates the risk of operator error occurring due to the increased focus in 
training using a simulator.  Corporately electric and gas operating errors are tracked closely and this 
position would help support operator error avoidance and the overall integrity of the natural gas 
transmission and distribution systems.  
 
With a dedicated employee overseeing the compliance of the CRM Plan, the Company will be able to 
enhance its ability to maintain compliance with state and federal regulations (e.g., 192.631 (h) and 
255.631 (h)) by being able to carefully managing the sections of the CRM Plan that address those 
requirements. 
 
Supports mitigation of a new Enterprise Risk Management (“ERM”), Large Scale Outage. 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
Continue with existing resources. This is rejected because currently the Company will not have proper 
personnel in place to train the Gas System Operators on the simulator in Gas SCADA which grows 
more complex as more information (data points) are coming into the Gas Control Center. In addition, 
there will not be enough resources to maintain the CRM plan for proper compliance and avoidance of 
violations per regular audits performed by the Department of Public Service.   
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1   
 
If no action is taken, the Company would be dependent upon existing resources to support 
compliance. The Company views this as inadequate.

Risk 3

in a mission critical department. Addition of the position will address the above-mentioned risks and 
requirements.

Work Description: 
The Company’s Gas Operations group is seeking to hire one (1) full-time employee to support the 
Contract Administration Group (“CAG”).  
 
The main functions of the new employee will include: 
1. Scheduler for gas and joint gas/electric projects, 
2. Maintain Ditch Repair Order (“DRO”) system and assign restoration orders to applicable restoration 
contractors, and 
3. Perform quality control checks on contractor completed gas construction paperwork for mapping 
and plastic pipe fusion documentation. 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
Gas Operation’s Contract Administration Group has had a temporary employee working on several 
functions for the last four years.  The work is not temporary and requires full time staffing to complete. 
 
The first function is scheduling Gas Operations work (including joint trench services with Electric 
Operations) to meet customer service dates.  This scheduling process has resulted in seamless work 
assignments and transparency in work being completed by Gas Operations, CAG, and New Business.   
 
The second function is reviewing DROs submitted by internal customers.  This review process ensures 
DROs are not duplicate requests and that they are complete, accurate, and assigned to the applicable 
restoration contractor.  Quickly and efficiently restoring customer landscaping and paving provides 
excellent customer service.   
 


--- Page 200 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 133 OF 141 
 
 
 
133 
The third function is performing quality control checks on Gas Operations contractor completed 
paperwork.  These reviews ensure that complete and accurate information is submitted to the 
Company’s Mapping department to have the new assets mapped and that documentation of 
polyethylene plastic joints meets PSC requirements.   
 
These three functions are critical in meeting internal and external customer needs and supporting 
personal, public, and gas asset safety. 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
n/a 
 
 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
These responsibilities could be divided among numerous people in multiple sections throughout Gas 
Operations but doing so would result in a loss of consistency and oversight over these functions. 
Therefore, this alternative was rejected. 
 
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
If there is no centralized oversight of scheduling gas service installation projects, this will result in 
inefficiencies and delays. This will lead to customer dissatisfaction.  
 
 
Risk 2 
If there is no centralized oversight of assigning Ditch Repair Orders, this will result in potential 
duplicate orders and delays in providing roadway and landscape restorations following construction 
projects. This will lead to dissatisfaction from both our customers and local municipalities.  
 
 
Risk 3 
If there is no centralized oversight of mapping and plastic fuse documentation, this could result in mis-
mapped facilities or incomplete fuse information for permanent records.

Project/Program Title: Night Shift Gas Operations Supervisor 
Project/Program Manager: Michael Casale

(Years/months) N/A 
Work Description: 
 
Gas Operations is seeking to hire a Gas Operations Supervisor for the Gas Service Department 
during the “off-hour” shift and/or “X” schedule, meaning that, this individual will provide 
supervisory oversight and meet supervisory expectations during off-hour shifts. A qualified 
“X” schedule Operations Supervisor is critical to performing our functions as a utility company 
in a safe manner during non-traditional business hours for both the individuals reporting to 
him/her and the general public. 
 
The “X” schedule Operations Supervisor responsibility will be inclusive of but not limited to 
managing operator qualified Troubleshooters/Servicemen during the late afternoon, 
overnight and weekend shifts.  
 
 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Operation of the Company’s gas system requires competent, professional, and qualified 
individuals readily available 24/7 to ensure the system operates in the manner in which it 
was designed. This resource request is required for operational safety and reliability. Events 
that are considered high consequence/low probability are at the forefront of Gas Operations 
future considerations. This position will provide a rapid response to such events with the 


--- Page 205 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (GIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 138 OF 141 
 
 
 
138 
expectation that measures will be taken to mitigate the extent of the risks associated with the 
initial impact.   
 
Currently, the “X” schedule function is performed by a limited number of Operating 
Supervisors from the Gas Service Department and the Gas Construction Department on a 
“stand-by” rotation. This rotation is not sustainable given the current environment as 
evidenced by several high profile gas incidents in the industry. The “stand-by” process 
involves having a Supervisor “on call” during their off hours and available to respond to an 
emergency should one occur. Timely response on stand-by is subject to external influences 
(such as distance), and these influences can have an adverse effect on suitable mitigation 
plans. A Supervisor on property will reduce risk by being able to immediately respond to an 
event. Especially since the Company has seen an increase in the number of Odor Calls over 
the last few years, going from 2018 to 2019 where the number of Odor Calls increased by 600.  
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
n/a 
 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
The alternative would be to continue to manage gas operations during non-traditional business hours 
through a “stand-by” process. This places significant strain on Company resources and could lead to a 
delay in being able to respond to an emergency quickly. 
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Alternative 3 description and reason for rejection 
 
 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
 
The risk of no action leaves the Gas Operations department vulnerable to delayed emergency response 
times. This risk has adverse effects not only on the reliability of the system, but also to the safety of 
employees and the public.  
 
 
Risk 2

*If whitepaper is supporting a capital project/program this refers to implementation O&M 
4. Definitions 
 
Total Funding Request: All funding requested for program or project over program/project lifecycle or 
for on-going programs the five-year requested amount, including all capital, O&M, retirement. 
 
Cost Savings: Reductions in costs that are currently being incurred (e.g., reduced annual maintenance 
cost relative to today) 
 
Cost Avoidance: Reductions in anticipated future costs that don’t occur today (e.g., anticipated short-
term fixes/maintenance if capital isn’t deployed) 
 
Project Status: 
 
• 
Initiation – New project, not authorized yet 
• 
Planning – Project authorized, not started yet 
• 
Executing – Project in-flight  
• 
On-going – Annual program
